Critical for harmonizing data from multiple healthcare systems using different measurement units.\n\n## Common Unit Conversions\n\n### Kidney Function Tests\n\n#### Serum Creatinine\n- **US Unit**: 15, likely in µmol/L\n\n#### BUN (Blood Urea Nitrogen)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.357\n- **Normal Range (US)**: 7-20 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (SI)**: 2.5-7.1 mmol/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 100, likely correct or truly elevated\n\n#### eGFR (Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate)\n- **Unit**: mL/min/1.73m²\n- **Normal suspicious\n\n#### Cystatin C\n- **US/SI Unit**: mg/L\n- **Normal Range**: 0.4-1.0 mg/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 5, might be another unit or truly elevated\n\n### Electrolytes and Minerals\n\n#### Sodium\n- **Unit**: mEq/L (equivalent to mmol/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 136-145 mEq/L\n- **Values < 100 or > 180 are suspicious**\n\n#### Potassium\n- **Unit**: mEq/L\n- **Normal Range**: 3.5-5.0 mEq/L\n- **Values < 1 or > 10 are suspicious**\n\n#### Chloride\n- **Unit**: mEq/L\n- **Normal Range**: 98-107 mEq/L\n- **Values < 50 or > 150 are suspicious**\n\n#### Bicarbonate (CO2)\n- **Unit**: mEq/L\n- **Normal Range**: 23-29 mEq/L\n- **Values < 10 or > 40 are suspicious**\n\n#### Magnesium\n- **Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.411 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 2.43)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 1.7-2.2 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 0.7-0.95 mmol/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 5, likely in µmol/L\n\n#### Calcium (Serum Total)\n- **Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.2495 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 4.008)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 8.5-10.2 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 2.12-2.55 mmol/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 15, likely in mmol/L\n\n#### Ionized Calcium\n- **Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: Same as total calcium\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 4.5-5.3 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 1.1-1.35 mmol/L\n\n#### Phosphorus\n- **Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.3229 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 3.097)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 2.5-4.5 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 0.81-1.45 mmol/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 8, likely correct or very elevated\n\n### Hormones and Protein Markers\n\n#### Intact PTH (Parathyroid Hormone)\n- **Unit**: pg/mL\n- **Normal Range**: 15-65 pg/mL (lab-dependent)\n- **CKD Target**: Often 2-9 times upper normal limit\n- **High value indicator**: Values > 500 are common in CKD but plausible\n\n#### Vitamin D 25-OH (Calcifediol)\n- **US Unit**: ng/mL\n- **SI Unit**: nmol/L\n- **Conversion**: nmol/L = ng/mL × 2.496 (or ng/mL = nmol/L × 0.4005)\n- **Normal Range (ng/mL)**: 30-100 ng/mL\n- **Normal Range (nmol/L)**: 75-250 nmol/L\n- **Deficiency**: < 20 ng/mL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 200, likely ng/mL\n\n#### Vitamin D 1,25-OH (Calcitriol)\n- **Unit**: pg/mL\n- **Alternative Unit**: pmol/L\n- **Conversion**: pmol/L = pg/mL × 2.6\n- **Normal Range (pg/mL)**: 19-67 pg/mL\n- **Normal Range (pmol/L)**: 49-174 pmol/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 100, might be in pmol/L\n\n### Hematology\n\n#### Hemoglobin\n- **US Unit**: g/dL\n- **SI Unit**: g/L\n- **Conversion**: g/L = g/dL × 10\n- **Normal Range (g/dL)**: Males 13.5-17.5, Females 12.0-15.5 g/dL\n- **Normal Range (g/L)**: Males 135-175, Females 120-155 g/L\n- **Target in CKD**: Usually > 10-11 g/dL (avoid > 13 g/dL)\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 25, likely g/dL; if < 25, could be g/L\n\n#### Hematocrit\n- **Unit**: % (percentage)\n- **Alternative interpretation**: Decimal (0.0-1.0)\n- **Normal Range**: 40-54% (males), 36-46% (females)\n- **Conversion**: If decimal given, multiply by 100\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 1, already in percentage; if < 1, likely decimal\n\n#### RBC Count\n- **Unit**: 10^6/µL (or 10^12/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 4.5-5.9 × 10^6/µL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 10, likely 10^6/µL\n\n#### WBC Count\n- **Unit**: 10^3/µL (or 10^9/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 4.5-11.0 × 10^3/µL\n- **Typical CKD**: Often slightly elevated\n\n#### Platelet Count\n- **Unit**: 10^3/µL (or 10^9/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 150-400 × 10^3/µL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 1000, likely in 10^3/µL\n\n### Iron Metabolism\n\n#### Serum Iron\n- **Unit**: µg/dL\n- **Alternative Unit**: µmol/L\n- **Conversion**: µmol/L = µg/dL × 0.1791\n- **Normal Range (µg/dL)**: 60-170 µg/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 300, likely µg/dL\n\n#### TIBC (Total Iron Binding Capacity)\n- **Unit**: µg/dL\n- **Normal Range**: 300-400 µg/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 600, suspicious\n\n#### Transferrin Saturation\n- **Unit**: %\n- **Normal Range**: 20-50%\n- **Note**: Should be calculated as (Serum Iron / TIBC) × 100\n\n#### Ferritin\n- **Unit**: ng/mL (or µg/L)\n- **Conversion**: ng/mL = µg/L (they're equivalent)\n- **Normal Range (ng/mL)**: Males 30-400, Females 15-200 ng/mL\n- **Target in CKD Anemia**: 100-800 ng/mL (or higher for some patients on ESA)\n- **High value indicator**: Values in thousands are plausible in CKD\n\n### Coagulation and Inflammation\n\n#### Reticulocyte Count\n- **Unit**: %\n- **Alternative Unit**: 10^9/L\n- **Normal Range**: 0.5-2.0%\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 100, likely already in percentage\n\n#### Bilirubin (Total and Direct)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: µmol/L\n- **Conversion**: µmol/L = mg/dL × 17.1\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 0.1-1.2 mg/dL (total), 0.0-0.3 mg/dL (direct)\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 30, likely mg/dL\n\n#### CRP (C-Reactive Protein)\n- **Unit**: mg/L\n- **Normal Range**: < 10 mg/L (or < 3 mg/L depending on assay)\n- **Typical CKD**: Often elevated\n- **High value indicator**: Values > 1000 are suspicious\n\n### Proteins\n\n#### Serum Albumin\n- **US Unit**: g/dL\n- **SI Unit**: g/L\n- **Conversion**: g/L = g/dL × 10\n- **Normal Range (g/dL)**: 3.5-5.0 g/dL\n- **Normal Range (g/L)**: 35-50 g/L\n- **CKD Concern**: < 3.5 g/dL indicates malnutrition\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 10, likely g/dL\n\n#### Total Protein\n- **US Unit**: g/dL\n- **SI Unit**: g/L\n- **Conversion**: g/L = g/dL × 10\n- **Normal Range (g/dL)**: 6.0-8.3 g/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 10, likely g/dL\n\n#### Prealbumin (Transthyretin)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: g/L\n- **Conversion**: g/L = mg/dL × 0.01 (or mg/dL = g/L × 100)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 20-40 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (g/L)**: 0.2-0.4 g/L\n- **Malnutrition indicator**: < 20 mg/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 50, likely mg/dL\n\n### Lipids\n\n#### Total Cholesterol, LDL, HDL, Triglycerides\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion Factor**:\n  - Cholesterol: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.0259 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 38.61)\n  - Triglycerides: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.01129 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 88.57)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**:\n  - Total: < 200\n  - LDL: < 100\n  - HDL: > 40 (male), > 50 (female)\n  - Triglycerides: < 150\n\n### Glucose and Glycemic Markers\n\n#### Glucose (Fasting)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.0555 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 18.02)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 70-100 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 3.9-5.6 mmol/L\n- **Diabetes**: ≥ 126 mg/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 800, likely mg/dL\n\n#### HbA1c\n- **Unit**: % (of total hemoglobin)\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/mol\n- **Conversion**: mmol/mol = (HbA1c% - 2.15) × 10.929\n- **Normal Range**: < 5.7%\n- **Diabetes**: ≥ 6.5%\n- **CKD Notes**: Less reliable in dialysis patients\n\n#### Fructosamine\n- **Unit**: µmol/L or mg/dL\n- **Normal Range**: 200-285 µmol/L\n- **Conversion** (if in mg/dL): µmol/L = mg/dL × 27.8\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, likely already in correct unit\n\n### Uric Acid\n\n#### Uric Acid (Serum)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: µmol/L\n- **Conversion**: µmol/L = mg/dL × 59.48\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: Males 3.5-7.2, Females 2.6-6.0 mg/dL\n- **CKD**: Often elevated\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 15, likely mg/dL\n\n### Urine Markers\n\n#### Urine Albumin, Creatinine, Protein\n- **Common Units**: mg/24h, mg/L, µg/min, mg/mg Cr\n- **UACR (Urine Albumin-to-Creatinine Ratio)**: mg/g (or mg/mmol)\n- **UPCR (Urine Protein-to-Creatinine Ratio)**: mg/g (or mg/mmol)\n- **Normal UACR**: < 30 mg/g\n- **Microalbuminuria**: 30-300 mg/g\n- **Macroalbuminuria**: > 300 mg/g\n- **High value indicator**: If UACR > 10000, might be in µg/mg instead of mg/g\n\n#### Urine pH\n- **Unit**: pH scale (0-14)\n- **Normal Range**: 4.5-8.0\n- **Typical**: 6.0-7.0\n\n#### Urine Specific Gravity\n- **Unit**: Dimensionless (1.000-1.030)\n- **Normal Range**: 1.005-1.030\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 2, likely incorrect format\n\n### Cardiac Markers\n\n#### BNP and NT-proBNP\n- **Unit**: pg/mL\n- **BNP Normal**: < 100 pg/mL\n- **NT-proBNP Normal**: < 125 pg/mL (age/sex dependent)\n- **CKD Baseline**: Often elevated\n- **High value indicator**: Very high values (> 5000) are plausible in CKD/ESRD\n\n#### Troponin I and T\n- **Unit**: ng/mL or pg/mL\n- **Normal**: < 0.04 ng/mL\n- **Elevation**: Suggests myocardial injury\n- **CKD**: Baseline elevation is common\n- **High value indicator**: Usually very low; > 1 is significant\n\n### Thyroid Function\n\n#### Free T4\n- **US Unit**: pg/mL\n- **SI Unit**: pmol/L\n- **Conversion**: pmol/L = pg/mL × 1.287\n- **Normal Range (pg/mL)**: 0.8-1.8 pg/mL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 5, likely pg/mL\n\n#### Free T3\n- **US Unit**: pg/mL\n- **SI Unit**: pmol/L\n- **Conversion**: pmol/L = pg/mL × 1.536\n- **Normal Range (pg/mL)**: 2.3-4.2 pg/mL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 10, likely pg/mL\n\n### Blood Gas\n\n#### Arterial pH\n- **Unit**: pH scale\n- **Normal Range**: 7.35-7.45\n- **CKD**: Often acidotic (< 7.35)\n\n#### pCO2 (Partial Pressure CO2)\n- **Unit**: mmHg\n- **Normal Range**: 35-45 mmHg\n- **Respiratory acidosis**: > 45 mmHg\n- **Respiratory alkalosis**: < 35 mmHg\n\n#### pO2 (Partial Pressure O2)\n- **Unit**: mmHg\n- **Normal Range**: 80-100 mmHg\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, suspicious\n\n#### Lactate\n- **Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Normal Range**: 0.5-2.0 mmol/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.1111\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 20, suspicious\n\n### Other Markers\n\n#### Beta-2 Microglobulin\n- **Unit**: mg/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: µg/mL (equivalent)\n- **Normal Range**: 0.6-2.4 mg/L\n- **CKD**: Accumulates significantly\n- **High value indicator**: Values in hundreds are plausible in dialysis patients\n\n#### Aluminum\n- **Unit**: µg/L\n- **Normal Range**: < 6 µg/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: µg/dL (= µmol/L)\n- **Conversion**: µg/dL = µg/L × 0.1\n- **Toxicity threshold**: > 100 µg/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, likely in µg/L\n\n#### Alkaline Phosphatase\n- **Unit**: U/L (or IU/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 30-120 U/L (age/sex dependent)\n- **High value indicator**: Values > 2000 are unusual but possible\n\n## Approach to Detection\n\n### Red Flags for Unit Mismatch\n1. **Value far outside physiological range** (> 3σ from mean)\n2. **Consistent pattern within feature** (e.g., all values too high)\n3. **Impossible values** (e.g., hemoglobin > 25 in g/dL, < 2 in g/L)\n4. **Relationship between related tests** (e.g., albumin too low relative to total protein)\n\n### Detection Strategy\n1. Calculate percentiles (5th, 25th, 75th, 95th) for each feature\n2. Identify outliers beyond physiological range\n3. Check if multiplying by conversion factor brings value into normal range\n4. Validate with related tests and clinical context\n\n## Example Detection and Conversion\n\n```python\n# Hemoglobin detection\nhem_values = [7.65, 10.75, 22.51, 15.5, ...]\n# 22.51 is suspicious for g/dL (max should be ~20)\n# But 22.51 g/L is low → maybe not converted properly\n# Check: 22.51 g/L ÷ 10 = 2.251 g/dL (too low)\n# Probably entry error; keep as is if in valid range\n\n# Serum Creatinine detection\ncreat_values = [7.616, 19.02, 14.693, 12.05, ...]\n# 19.02 mg/dL is very high (normal ~0.8-1.2)\n# 19.02 ÷ 88.4 = 0.215 mg/dL (normal)\n# But 19.02 × 88.4 = 1682 µmol/L (too high)\n# Likely already in mg/dL or error\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document assumptions** about unit identification\n2. **Keep audit trail** of conversions applied\n3. **Cross-validate** with related markers (e.g., creatinine with eGFR)\n4. **Handle edge cases** (missing values, truly extreme values from disease severity)\n5. **Consider clinical context** (CKD patients have different normal ranges)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1172,"cache_read_input_tokens":29938,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1172,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0baed222-1a6d-4058-b595-9f5d3556128c","uuid":"5a7b81d4-8204-4377-8027-2bc536095c4e"}

 - Triglycerides: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.01129 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 88.57)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**:\n  - Total: < 200\n  - LDL: < 100\n  - HDL: > 40 (male), > 50 (female)\n  - Triglycerides: < 150\n\n### Glucose and Glycemic Markers\n\n#### Glucose (Fasting)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.0555 (or mg/dL = mmol/L × 18.02)\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: 70-100 mg/dL\n- **Normal Range (mmol/L)**: 3.9-5.6 mmol/L\n- **Diabetes**: ≥ 126 mg/dL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 800, likely mg/dL\n\n#### HbA1c\n- **Unit**: % (of total hemoglobin)\n- **Alternative Unit**: mmol/mol\n- **Conversion**: mmol/mol = (HbA1c% - 2.15) × 10.929\n- **Normal Range**: < 5.7%\n- **Diabetes**: ≥ 6.5%\n- **CKD Notes**: Less reliable in dialysis patients\n\n#### Fructosamine\n- **Unit**: µmol/L or mg/dL\n- **Normal Range**: 200-285 µmol/L\n- **Conversion** (if in mg/dL): µmol/L = mg/dL × 27.8\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, likely already in correct unit\n\n### Uric Acid\n\n#### Uric Acid (Serum)\n- **US Unit**: mg/dL\n- **SI Unit**: µmol/L\n- **Conversion**: µmol/L = mg/dL × 59.48\n- **Normal Range (mg/dL)**: Males 3.5-7.2, Females 2.6-6.0 mg/dL\n- **CKD**: Often elevated\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 15, likely mg/dL\n\n### Urine Markers\n\n#### Urine Albumin, Creatinine, Protein\n- **Common Units**: mg/24h, mg/L, µg/min, mg/mg Cr\n- **UACR (Urine Albumin-to-Creatinine Ratio)**: mg/g (or mg/mmol)\n- **UPCR (Urine Protein-to-Creatinine Ratio)**: mg/g (or mg/mmol)\n- **Normal UACR**: < 30 mg/g\n- **Microalbuminuria**: 30-300 mg/g\n- **Macroalbuminuria**: > 300 mg/g\n- **High value indicator**: If UACR > 10000, might be in µg/mg instead of mg/g\n\n#### Urine pH\n- **Unit**: pH scale (0-14)\n- **Normal Range**: 4.5-8.0\n- **Typical**: 6.0-7.0\n\n#### Urine Specific Gravity\n- **Unit**: Dimensionless (1.000-1.030)\n- **Normal Range**: 1.005-1.030\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 2, likely incorrect format\n\n### Cardiac Markers\n\n#### BNP and NT-proBNP\n- **Unit**: pg/mL\n- **BNP Normal**: < 100 pg/mL\n- **NT-proBNP Normal**: < 125 pg/mL (age/sex dependent)\n- **CKD Baseline**: Often elevated\n- **High value indicator**: Very high values (> 5000) are plausible in CKD/ESRD\n\n#### Troponin I and T\n- **Unit**: ng/mL or pg/mL\n- **Normal**: < 0.04 ng/mL\n- **Elevation**: Suggests myocardial injury\n- **CKD**: Baseline elevation is common\n- **High value indicator**: Usually very low; > 1 is significant\n\n### Thyroid Function\n\n#### Free T4\n- **US Unit**: pg/mL\n- **SI Unit**: pmol/L\n- **Conversion**: pmol/L = pg/mL × 1.287\n- **Normal Range (pg/mL)**: 0.8-1.8 pg/mL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 5, likely pg/mL\n\n#### Free T3\n- **US Unit**: pg/mL\n- **SI Unit**: pmol/L\n- **Conversion**: pmol/L = pg/mL × 1.536\n- **Normal Range (pg/mL)**: 2.3-4.2 pg/mL\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 10, likely pg/mL\n\n### Blood Gas\n\n#### Arterial pH\n- **Unit**: pH scale\n- **Normal Range**: 7.35-7.45\n- **CKD**: Often acidotic (< 7.35)\n\n#### pCO2 (Partial Pressure CO2)\n- **Unit**: mmHg\n- **Normal Range**: 35-45 mmHg\n- **Respiratory acidosis**: > 45 mmHg\n- **Respiratory alkalosis**: < 35 mmHg\n\n#### pO2 (Partial Pressure O2)\n- **Unit**: mmHg\n- **Normal Range**: 80-100 mmHg\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, suspicious\n\n#### Lactate\n- **Unit**: mmol/L\n- **Normal Range**: 0.5-2.0 mmol/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: mg/dL\n- **Conversion**: mmol/L = mg/dL × 0.1111\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 20, suspicious\n\n### Other Markers\n\n#### Beta-2 Microglobulin\n- **Unit**: mg/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: µg/mL (equivalent)\n- **Normal Range**: 0.6-2.4 mg/L\n- **CKD**: Accumulates significantly\n- **High value indicator**: Values in hundreds are plausible in dialysis patients\n\n#### Aluminum\n- **Unit**: µg/L\n- **Normal Range**: < 6 µg/L\n- **Alternative Unit**: µg/dL (= µmol/L)\n- **Conversion**: µg/dL = µg/L × 0.1\n- **Toxicity threshold**: > 100 µg/L\n- **High value indicator**: If value > 500, likely in µg/L\n\n#### Alkaline Phosphatase\n- **Unit**: U/L (or IU/L)\n- **Normal Range**: 30-120 U/L (age/sex dependent)\n- **High value indicator**: Values > 2000 are unusual but possible\n\n## Approach to Detection\n\n### Red Flags for Unit Mismatch\n1. **Value far outside physiological range** (> 3σ from mean)\n2. **Consistent pattern within feature** (e.g., all values too high)\n3. **Impossible values** (e.g., hemoglobin > 25 in g/dL, < 2 in g/L)\n4. **Relationship between related tests** (e.g., albumin too low relative to total protein)\n\n### Detection Strategy\n1. Calculate percentiles (5th, 25th, 75th, 95th) for each feature\n2. Identify outliers beyond physiological range\n3. Check if multiplying by conversion factor brings value into normal range\n4. Validate with related tests and clinical context\n\n## Example Detection and Conversion\n\n```python\n# Hemoglobin detection\nhem_values = [7.65, 10.75, 22.51, 15.5, ...]\n# 22.51 is suspicious for g/dL (max should be ~20)\n# But 22.51 g/L is low → maybe not converted properly\n# Check: 22.51 g/L ÷ 10 = 2.251 g/dL (too low)\n# Probably entry error; keep as is if in valid range\n\n# Serum Creatinine detection\ncreat_values = [7.616, 19.02, 14.693, 12.05, ...]\n# 19.02 mg/dL is very high (normal ~0.8-1.2)\n# 19.02 ÷ 88.4 = 0.215 mg/dL (normal)\n# But 19.02 × 88.4 = 1682 µmol/L (too high)\n# Likely already in mg/dL or error\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document assumptions** about unit identification\n2. **Keep audit trail** of conversions applied\n3. **Cross-validate** with related markers (e.g., creatinine with eGFR)\n4. **Handle edge cases** (missing values, truly extreme values from disease severity)\n5. **Consider clinical context** (CKD patients have different normal ranges)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Shows min, 25%, 50%, 75%, max, mean, std\n\n# More detailed statistics\ndf[numeric_cols].quantile([0.01, 0.05, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.95, 0.99])\n\n# Identify columns with extreme values\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    q1 = df[col].quantile(0.25)\n    q3 = df[col].quantile(0.75)\n    iqr = q3 - q1\n    lower_bound = q1 - 1.5 * iqr\n    upper_bound = q3 + 1.5 * iqr\n    outliers = df[(df[col] < lower_bound) | (df[col] > upper_bound)]\n    if len(outliers) > 0:\n        print(f\"{col}: {len(outliers)} outliers\")\n```\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats\n\n# Calculate z-scores\nz_scores = np.abs(stats.zscore(df[numeric_cols], nan_policy='omit'))\n\n# Find rows with extreme z-scores (> 3 or > 5)\noutlier_rows = (z_scores > 3).any(axis=1)\ndf_outliers = df[outlier_rows]\n```\n\n#### IQR Method\n```python\n# Interquartile Range based detection\ndef find_outliers_iqr(col):\n    q1 = col.quantile(0.25)\n    q3 = col.quantile(0.75)\n    iqr = q3 - q1\n    lower = q1 - 1.5 * iqr\n    upper = q3 + 1.5 * iqr\n    return (col < lower) | (col > upper)\n\n# Apply to each column\noutlier_mask = pd.DataFrame()\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    outlier_mask[col] = find_outliers_iqr(df[col])\n```\n\n### Domain-Specific Outlier Detection\n\n#### Physiological Range Validation\n```python\n# Define normal ranges for each parameter\nnormal_ranges = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': (0.6, 1.2),\n    'Hemoglobin': (10, 20),\n    'Glucose': (50, 500),\n    # ... more ranges\n}\n\n# Flag values outside ranges\ndef flag_abnormal(col, value, ranges):\n    if col not in ranges:\n        return False\n    min_val, max_val = ranges[col]\n    return value < min_val or value > max_val\n\n# Check all values\nabnormal_mask = {}\nfor col, (min_val, max_val) in normal_ranges.items():\n    if col in df.columns:\n        abnormal_mask[col] = (df[col] < min_val) | (df[col] > max_val)\n```\n\n#### Ratio-Based Validation\n```python\n# Relationships between related tests\n# For example: PTH should correlate with calcium, phosphorus\n# Albumin should be < Total Protein\n# BUN should be higher than Creatinine (as ratio of ~10-20)\n\ndef validate_bun_creatinine_ratio(bun, creatinine):\n    \"\"\"BUN/Creatinine ratio typically 10-20\"\"\"\n    if creatinine == 0:\n        return None\n    ratio = bun / creatinine\n    return 8 < ratio < 25  # Lenient bounds\n\n# Apply validation\ndf['bun_creat_valid'] = df.apply(\n    lambda row: validate_bun_creatinine_ratio(row['BUN'], row['Serum_Creatinine']),\n    axis=1\n)\n```\n\n## Missing Data Handling\n\n### Identifying Missing Values\n```python\n# Count missing per column\nmissing_counts = df.isna().sum()\nmissing_pct = (missing_counts / len(df) * 100).sort_values(ascending=False)\n\n# Count missing per row\nmissing_per_row = df.isna().sum(axis=1)\n\n# Visualize missing data\nprint(df.isna().sum())\nprint(f\"Total missing: {df.isna().sum().sum()}\")\n\n# Find rows with > threshold missing\nthreshold = 10\nproblematic_rows = df[missing_per_row > threshold]\n```\n\n### Dropping Incomplete Records\n```python\n# Drop rows with ANY missing values (strict)\ndf_complete = df.dropna(how='any')\n\n# Drop rows with missing in key columns\nkey_cols = ['patient_id', 'Serum_Creatinine', 'BUN', 'eGFR']\ndf_complete = df.dropna(subset=key_cols, how='any')\n\n# Track what was dropped\noriginal_count = len(df)\nfinal_count = len(df_complete)\ndropped_count = original_count - final_count\nprint(f\"Dropped {dropped_count} rows ({dropped_count/original_count*100:.1f}%)\")\n```\n\n### Identifying Unusual Missing Patterns\n```python\n# Missing completely at random vs. systematic\n# Check if missingness correlates with other variables\nmissing_by_patient = df.groupby('patient_id').apply(lambda x: x.isna().sum().sum())\n\n# If certain patients have more missing data\npatients_with_high_missing = missing_by_patient[missing_by_patient > 20]\n\n# Columns that frequently have missing data together\ncorrelation_missing = df.isna().corr()\n```\n\n## Data Format Issues\n\n### Detecting Format Problems\n\n#### Scientific Notation Issues\n```python\n# Scientific notation appears as very small or very large numbers\n# When converted from string: \"1.23e2\" → 123.0\n\n# Check for suspicious small values that might be in scientific notation\nsmall_values = df[df['Hemoglobin'] < 1]  # Hemoglobin should be > 7\n\n# Check for values that look like they were incorrectly parsed\nvery_large = df[df['Glucose'] > 1000]  # Glucose > 1000 suspicious\n```\n\n#### Comma as Decimal Separator\n```python\n# After reading CSV, commas may remain in numeric strings\n# \"12,34\" should become 12.34\n\n# Detect: try converting to float, catch errors\ndef has_comma_decimal(val):\n    val_str = str(val)\n    return ',' in val_str and val_str.count('.') == 0\n\n# Find columns with this issue\nfor col in df.columns[1:]:  # Skip patient_id\n    comma_count = df[col].apply(\n        lambda x: ',' in str(x) if isinstance(x, str) else False\n    ).sum()\n    if comma_count > 0:\n        print(f\"{col}: {comma_count} values with commas\")\n```\n\n#### Decimal Place Inconsistency\n```python\n# Check for varying decimal places\ndef get_decimal_places(val):\n    val_str = str(val)\n    if '.' in val_str:\n        return len(val_str.split('.')[1])\n    elif ',' in val_str:\n        return len(val_str.split(',')[1])\n    return 0\n\n# Analyze decimal places per column\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    decimal_places = df[col].apply(get_decimal_places)\n    print(f\"{col}: {decimal_places.value_counts().to_dict()}\")\n```\n\n## Data Transformation Validation\n\n### Before/After Comparison\n```python\n# Compare distributions before and after cleaning\ndef compare_distributions(original, cleaned, col_name):\n    print(f\"\\n{col_name}:\")\n    print(f\"  Original - min: {original.min():.2f}, max: {original.max():.2f}, mean: {original.mean():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Cleaned  - min: {cleaned.min():.2f}, max: {cleaned.max():.2f}, mean: {cleaned.mean():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Rows removed: {len(original) - len(cleaned)}\")\n\n# Apply to all columns\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    compare_distributions(df_original[col], df_cleaned[col], col)\n```\n\n### Quality Assurance Checks\n```python\n# After all transformations, verify data quality\n\n# 1. No scientific notation\nscientific_notation_count = 0\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    for val in df[col]:\n        if 'e' in str(val).lower():\n            scientific_notation_count += 1\nprint(f\"Scientific notation remaining: {scientific_notation_count}\")\n\n# 2. No commas in numeric values\ncomma_count = 0\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    for val in df[col]:\n        if ',' in str(val):\n            comma_count += 1\nprint(f\"Commas remaining: {comma_count}\")\n\n# 3. All values within physiological ranges (with tolerance)\nout_of_range = 0\nfor col, (min_val, max_val) in normal_ranges.items():\n    if col in df.columns:\n        out_of_range += ((df[col] < min_val) | (df[col] > max_val)).sum()\nprint(f\"Values outside physiological range: {out_of_range}\")\n\n# 4. Exactly 2 decimal places in output format\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    for val in df[col]:\n        val_str = str(val)\n        if '.' in val_str:\n            decimals = len(val_str.split('.')[1])\n            if decimals != 2:\n                print(f\"  {col}: {val} has {decimals} decimals, not 2\")\n```\n\n### Comprehensive Data Quality Report\n```python\ndef generate_quality_report(df, original_df):\n    report = {\n        'original_rows': len(original_df),\n        'final_rows': len(df),\n        'rows_dropped': len(original_df) - len(df),\n        'rows_dropped_pct': (len(original_df) - len(df)) / len(original_df) * 100,\n        'missing_values': df.isna().sum().sum(),\n        'scientific_notation': 0,\n        'commas_in_values': 0,\n        'decimal_places_error': 0,\n    }\n\n    # Count format errors\n    for col in df.columns[1:]:  # Skip patient_id\n        for val in df[col]:\n            val_str = str(val)\n            if 'e' in val_str.lower():\n                report['scientific_notation'] += 1\n            if ',' in val_str:\n                report['commas_in_values'] += 1\n            if '.' in val_str:\n                decimals = len(val_str.split('.')[1])\n                if decimals != 2:\n                    report['decimal_places_error'] += 1\n\n    return report\n\nquality = generate_quality_report(df_final, df_original)\nfor key, value in quality.items():\n    print(f\"{key}: {value}\")\n```\n\n## Unit Conversion Validation\n\n### Post-Conversion Checks\n```python\n# After converting suspicious values, verify results\n\n# 1. Check consistency with related markers\ndef validate_consistency(df):\n    # eGFR should correlate inversely with Creatinine\n    corr = df[['Serum_Creatinine', 'eGFR']].corr().iloc[0, 1]\n    print(f\"Creatinine-eGFR correlation: {corr:.3f}\")  # Should be negative\n\n    # BUN/Creatinine ratio should be reasonable\n    ratio = df['BUN'] / df['Serum_Creatinine']\n    print(f\"BUN/Creatinine ratio - mean: {ratio.mean():.1f}, std: {ratio.std():.1f}\")\n\nvalidate_consistency(df_cleaned)\n```\n\n## Pandas Features for Validation\n\n### Using apply() for Row-Wise Operations\n```python\n# Check multiple conditions per row\ndef validate_row(row):\n    errors = []\n    if row['Serum_Creatinine'] < 0.3 or row['Serum_Creatinine'] > 30:\n        errors.append('Creatinine out of range')\n    if row['Hemoglobin'] < 3 or row['Hemoglobin'] > 25:\n        errors.append('Hemoglobin out of range')\n    return len(errors) == 0\n\ndf['is_valid'] = df.apply(validate_row, axis=1)\ninvalid_rows = df[~df['is_valid']]\n```\n\n### Handling Edge Cases\n```python\n# Some tests have valid extreme values in CKD\n# PTH, BNP can be very high\n# Creatinine can be > 10 in ESRD\n\n# Create more lenient ranges\nliberal_ranges = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': (0.5, 50),  # Very high in ESRD\n    'Intact_PTH': (1, 5000),  # Can be very elevated\n    'BNP': (1, 50000),  # CKD specific\n    'NT_proBNP': (1, 100000),\n}\n\n# Use these for CKD data, not standard clinical ranges\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5421,"cache_read_input_tokens":31110,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5421,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0baed222-1a6d-4058-b595-9f5d3556128c","uuid":"50e47658-e4c3-4059-8218-cf68e5a758ba"}
